j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}
in
\mathcal{M}_{o}
has
j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}
as its nearest neighbour in
\mathcal{M}_{n}
, i.e., the nearest neighbour of the nearest neighbour of each node
j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}
is itself, following our notation:

(11)

\displaystyle\forall j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}},\quad i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\leftarrow j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\implies i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\rightarrow j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}.

In such case, the transform can be simplified (see the proof in
Appendix
A.3
) as:

(12)

\displaystyle\tilde{W}^{e}_{ij_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\forall k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\text{ s.t. }k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\rightarrow j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}W^{e}_{ik_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}},

\displaystyle\tilde{W}^{d}_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{\forall k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\text{ s.t. }k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\rightarrow i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}{\operatorname{mean}}W^{d}_{k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}j},

\displaystyle\tilde{b}^{d}_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{\forall k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\text{ s.t. }k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\rightarrow i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}{\operatorname{mean}}b^{d}_{k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}.

We note that the absence of leftward arrows means we are only required to find nearest neighbours of nodes in the mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
and not those in
\mathcal{M}_{n}
, reducing the algorithmic time complexity to
O(N_{o}\log N_{n})
. The implementation of the expansive transformation with this complexity is shown in
Algorithm
2
.

Algorithm 2

Simplified GFN transform in the agglomerative case, as given in
Equation 12
.

function

Agglomerate
(
{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times N_{o}},{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{o}\times L},{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{o}},\mathcal{M}_{o},\mathcal{M}_{n}
)

Initialise
\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{e}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times N_{n}},\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{n}\times L},\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{n}}

\operatorname{counts}\leftarrow\bm{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{n}}

for

i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}=1\text{ to }N_{o}

do

j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\leftarrow\operatorname{nearest-neighbour}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\mathcal{M}_{o}\left[i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\right])

\operatorname{counts}[j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}]\leftarrow\operatorname{counts}[j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}]+1

\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{e}\left[:,\penalty\ j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\right]\leftarrow{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e}\left[:,\penalty\ i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\right]

\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{d}\left[j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}},\penalty\ :\right]\leftarrow\left(\left(\operatorname{counts}[j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.83623pt}{\resizebox{582870}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.83623pt}{\resizebox{582870}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{555277}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{636725}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}]-1\right)\tilde{\bm{W}}^{d}\left[j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}},\penalty\ :\right]+{\bm{W}}^{d}\left[i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}},\penalty\ :\right]\right)\mathbin{/}\operatorname{counts}[j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}]

\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}}^{d}\left[j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\right]\leftarrow\left(\left(\operatorname{counts}[j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}]-1\right)\tilde{\bm{b}}^{d}\left[j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\right]+{\bm{b}}^{d}\left[i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\right]\right)\mathbin{/}\operatorname{counts}[j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}]

end

for

return

\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{e},\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{d},\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}}^{d}

end

function

5.2.3.
Computational advantages

For hierarchical meshes, it follows that if
\mathcal{M}_{o}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{n}
then the transform
\mathcal{M}_{o}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}
is expansive, whereas if
\mathcal{M}_{n}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{o}
then the transform
\mathcal{M}_{o}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}
is agglomerative. Moreover, if the transform is both expansive and agglomerative then it must hold that
\forall i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}
,
i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\longleftrightarrow j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}
and
\forall j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}
,
i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\longleftrightarrow j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}
, i.e. there is a one-to-one correspondence between nodes in the original mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
and in the new mesh
\mathcal{M}_{n}
, and the GFN transforms reduce to nearest neighbour interpolation of the weights.

However, for general meshes a transform can be both, neither or one of expansive and agglomerative. Despite this, notably any general GFN transform can be expressed as a succession of an expansive update and an agglomerative update, (proof in
Appendix
A.5
) i.e.
Equation 5
is equivalent to defining the new mesh

(13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{o}\cup\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\left[i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\right]\text{ s.t. }j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\leftarrow i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\text{ but not }j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\rightarrow and computing the transform in two steps, as

step}

The general GFN algorithm can thus be easily expressed as in
Algorithm
3
, with a demonstrative illustration of this interpretation in
Figure 7
.

Algorithm 3

General GFN transform leveraging an expansion step followed by an agglomerative update, as given in
Equation 5
.

function

GFN
(
{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times N_{o}},{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{n}\times L},{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{o}},\mathcal{M}_{o},\mathcal{M}_{n}
)

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{o}\cup\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\left[i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\right]\text{ s.t. }j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\leftarrow i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\text{ but not }j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\rightarrow i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\}

\hat{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{e},\hat{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{d},\hat{{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}}^{d}\leftarrow\textsc{Expand}({\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e},{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d},{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{d},\mathcal{M}_{o},\mathcal{M})

\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{e},\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{d},\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}}^{d}\leftarrow\textsc{Agglomerate}(\hat{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{e},\hat{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{d},\hat{{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}}^{d},\mathcal{M},\mathcal{M}_{n})

return

\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{e},\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{d},\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}}^{d}

end

function

Figure 7
.
The GFN transform can be thought about as applying an expansive update followed by an agglomerative update.

6.
Graph Feedforward Network Reduced Order Model (GFN-ROM)

Having defined GFNs as a means of evaluating trained autoencoders on multifidelity data, we leverage them to propose a reduced order model capable of being evaluated on arbitrary meshes.
As depicted in
Figure 8
, the proposed GFN-ROM architecture consists of two components: an autoencoder and a mapper. The autoencoder is based on GFNs, while the mapper can be any neural network model which maps the fixed-size vector parameters directly to the fixed-size latent representation. Notice that, within this parametric context, the mapper does not need to be defined using GFNs since it does not deal with graph-structured data.

GFN-ROM is inspired by recent state-of-the-art autoencoder-based models such as DL-ROM
[
33
]
, which uses standard convolutions, and GCA-ROM
[
77
]
, which uses graph convolutions. In fact, GFN-ROM can be regarded as an extension of such methods. Like both models, we train the GFN-ROM model based on a weighted sum of two losses: the reconstruction loss and the mapper loss.
Given a GFN-ROM model with weights defined on a mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
, the per-example loss functions are defined for the
t
th training sample
\left(\bm{\mu}_{t},\penalty\ {\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\bm{\mu}_{t})\right)
, defined on the
t
-th mesh
\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}
and for the
t
-th parameter value
\bm{\mu}_{t}
, as

(16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{recon}}[\bm{\mu}_{t},{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\bm{\mu}_{t})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}|}||\operatorname{dec}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}}\left(\operatorname{enc}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}}({\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\bm{\mu}_{t}))\right)-{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\bm{\mu}_{t})||_{2}^{2},

(17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{map}}[\bm{\mu}_{t},{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\bm{\mu}_{t})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}||\operatorname{enc}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}}({\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\bm{\mu}_{t}))-\operatorname{map}(\bm{\mu}_{t})||_{2}^{2}.

The overall loss is given as a weighted mean of these losses over a set
\{\left(\bm{\mu}_{t},\penalty\ {\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\bm{\mu}_{t})\right)\}_{t=1}^{T}
of
T
training examples

(18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}|}{\sum_{s=1}^{T}|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{s}|}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\text{recon}}[\bm{\mu}_{t},{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\bm{\mu}_{t})]+\omega\mathcal{L}_{\text{map}}[\bm{\mu}_{t},{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\bm{\mu}_{t})]\right),

where we have introduced the mapper weight hyperparameter
\omega
.
We note that at inference, the encoder is no longer required and, for a parameter
\bm{\mu}_{t}
and mesh
\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}
, a GFN-ROM model with weights defined on a mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
predicts
\operatorname{dec}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}}(\operatorname{map}(\bm{\mu}_{t}))
.

Figure 8
.
GFN-ROM architecture, consisting of an autoencoder and a mapper. During inference, predictions can be computed by decoding the mapper output.

6.1.
Main features of the architecture

The proposed GFN-ROM architecture benefits from a number of desirable properties that we discuss in the following sections, which make it an attractive tool for applications with multifidelity data.

6.1.1.
Self-consistency

The GFN transforms are self-consistent approaches, meaning that a series of two transforms from
\mathcal{M}_{o}
to
\mathcal{M}_{n}
and then back to
\mathcal{M}_{o}
recovers exactly the original weights
{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e},{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d},{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{d}
provided that the transform
\mathcal{M}_{o}
to
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is expansive, i.e.

{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e},{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d},{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{d}=\operatorname{GFN}^{\mathcal{M}_{n}\to\mathcal{M}_{o}}(\operatorname{GFN}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}}({\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e},{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d},{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{d})).

The self-consistency, a proof of which is given in
Appendix
A.4
, is a marked advantage in comparison to any other interpolation methods such as Kriging, linear regression, spline, and
k
NN (with the exception of the case
k=1
). This means that no information is lost during the expansive transform and the original weights can be directly recovered, unlike for other interpolation-based approaches.

6.1.2.
Bound on super- and sub-resolution error of GFN-ROM

GFN-ROM is a resolution-invariant architecture, meaning one is often interested in evaluating the model on a finer discretisation (super-resolution) or coarser discretisation (sub-resolution) compared to a reference resolution. A particularly attractive feature of the architecture is that one can bound the performance on any testing fidelity given certain information. Specifically, given a bound
\delta
on the difference between the features of nearest neighbours, and a bound
\tau
on the performance of GFN-ROM on the known mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
, i.e.
4
4

4

We use the notation
u(x_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}})
, equivalent to
u_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}
, in order to emphasise the dependence of
u
on spatial position.

\displaystyle\forall (20)

\displaystyle\forall i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}},\quad\left|u(x_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}})-\operatorname{dec}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{o}}(\operatorname{map}(\bm{\mu}))_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}\right|\leq\tau,

one can show that the performance of GFN-ROM on the new mesh
\mathcal{M}_{n}
can be bounded as:

(21)

\displaystyle\forall i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}},\quad\left|u(x_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}})-\operatorname{dec}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}}(\operatorname{map}(\bm{\mu}))_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}\right|\leq\tau+\delta.

A proof is given in
Appendix
A.6
. We note that there is no dependence at all on the weights and biases, only on the mesh similarities and the GFN-ROM performance on the reference resolution.

6.1.3.
Bound on super- and sub-resolution error of the GFN-ROM mapper

Given the same bound
\delta
as in
Equation 19
, and a bound
\alpha
on the performance of the mapper on the known mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
, i.e.

(22)

\displaystyle\forall i,\quad\left|\operatorname{map}(\bm{\mu})_{i}-\operatorname{enc}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{o}}({\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}})_{i}\right|\leq\alpha,

one can show that the performance of the mapper on the new mesh
\mathcal{M}_{n}
can be bounded as:

(23)

\displaystyle\forall i,\quad\left|\operatorname{map}(\bm{\mu})_{i}-\operatorname{enc}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}}({\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}})_{i}\right|\leq\alpha+\delta C^{P+1}||{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e}||_{\infty}.

A proof is given in
Appendix
A.7
.

one can show that the performance of the autoencoder on the new mesh

(25)

\displaystyle\forall i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}},\quad\left|u(x_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}})-\operatorname{dec}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}}(\operatorname{enc}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}}({\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}))_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}\right|\leq\beta+\delta+\delta C^{Q+1}||{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d}||_{\infty}||{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e}||_{\infty}.

A proof is given in
Appendix
A.8
. The dependence on the norm of the weights in the bound suggests that regularisation is important to allow for good generalisation for the autoencoder.

6.2.
Link between GFN-ROM and novel architectures

Following the discussion in
Section
4.2
and
Section
4.3
, we discuss here how GFN-ROM is connected with state-of-the-art resolution invariant approaches.

6.2.1.
GNNs and GFN-ROM

Concerning graph neural networks, we can interpret a GFN encoder layer and a GFN decoder layer as two different types of GNN: namely, a graph pooling layer and a graph unpooling layer, respectively. We can consider the input to the autoencoder, which is some PDE solution
u(x,\bm{\mu})
evaluated on some discretized domain, as a fully connected graph. The GFN encoder layer is then tasked with computing a vector representation summarising the input graph, i.e. a graph pooling task. The GFN decoder layer has precisely the opposite task of reconstructing a graph given a summarised representation i.e. graph unpooling. At inference, the overall GFN-ROM architecture can thus be thought of as a graph unpooling model, where the PDE solution is identified by the parameters
\bm{\mu}
.

6.2.2.
NOs and GFN-ROM

A parameterised PDE with a parameter
a\in\mathcal{A}
and solution
u\in\mathcal{U}
, where
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{U}
are Banach spaces, defines a mapping
\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{U}
, i.e.
u(x)=\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}[a](x)
. A neural operator aims to directly approximate this mapping
\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}
by means of an operator
\mathcal{G}_{\theta}\approx\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}
with trainable parameters
\theta
.
A single-layer neural operator can be written as

\displaystyle G_{\theta}=\mathcal{Q}\circ\sigma(W+\mathcal{K}+b)\circ\mathcal{P},

where
\mathcal{P}
,
\mathcal{Q}
are the local lifting and projection mappings,
W
is a local linear operator (matrix),
\mathcal{K}
is an integral kernel operator,
b
is a bias function and
\sigma
is a pointwise activation function. We refer to Kovachki
et al.
for further details
[
55
]
. Considering a simplified neural operator with
W=0
and no lifting or projection mappings, we obtain

(26)

\displaystyle G_{\theta}[v](y)=\sigma\left(\int_{D}\kappa(x,y)v(x)\,\mathrm{d}x+b(y)\right),

where
\kappa
is a kernel function and
D
is the domain of integration. Usually, one does not have complete access to the function
v(x)
, but only to its evaluations at certain points, meaning this integral has to be numerically computed. Supposing we are given function evaluations on a mesh
\mathcal{M}
, the numerical approximation of the integral reads as

(27)

\displaystyle G_{\theta}[v](y)\approx\sigma\left(\sum_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{458491}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{458491}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{351656}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{351658}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}h_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{458491}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{458491}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{351656}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{351658}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}\kappa(x_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{458491}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{458491}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{351656}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{351658}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}}},y)v(x_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{458491}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{458491}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{351656}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{351658}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}}})+b(y)\right),

where
h_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{458491}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{458491}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{351656}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{351658}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}
is a factor depending on the integration method.

GFN-ROM is closely related to NOs. In fact, in the expansive case, GFN-ROM can be recast as a particular instance of NOs, since we can directly obtain both the encoder and decoder. To make the link with GFN-ROM, we start with the mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
, the associated weights and biases (
{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e}
,
{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{e}
,
{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d}
,
{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{d}
), and choose a particular kernel
\kappa
.

Starting from
Equation 26
, discarding the
y
-dependence and taking
D=\Omega
,
b=b^{e}_{i}
, and
\kappa(x)=W^{e}_{ik_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}/|B_{k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}|

, one can write

We abuse notation to write

\displaystyle G_{\theta}[u]

h_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\}\rvert}u_{j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}+b^{e}_{i}\right),\qquad\text{ where}\quad k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\leftarrow j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}},

\displaystyle=\operatorname{enc}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}}({\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}})_{i}.

Similarly, the decoder can be obtained from
Equation 26
without any
x
-dependence.
Taking
\tilde{\kappa}=|D|\kappa
, setting
\sigma
to the identity function,
\tilde{\kappa}(y)={\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d}_{k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}},:}
where
k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\leftarrow y
, and
b(y)=b^{d}_{k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}
where
k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\leftarrow y
, one obtains

(30)

\displaystyle G_{\theta}[{\mbox{\boldmath{$z$}}}](y)

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\int_{D}\kappa(y){\mbox{\boldmath{$z$}}}\,\mathrm{d}x+b(y)\right),

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\tilde{\kappa}(y){\mbox{\boldmath{$z$}}}+b(y)\right),

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{L}{W}^{d}_{k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}j}z_{j}+{b}^{d}_{k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}},\qquad k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{827776}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{785437}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{888616}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\leftarrow y.

Considering
y=\mathcal{M}_{n}[i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.25435pt}{\resizebox{874305}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31706pt}{\resizebox{832916}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{955087}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}]
, we recover the GFN-ROM decoder in the expansive case as

(31)

\displaystyle G_{\theta}[{\mbox{\boldmath{$z$}}}](y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{L}{W}^{d}_{k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}j}z_{j}+{b}^{d}_{k_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{627905}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{539283}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{614336}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{o}\mathstrut$}}}}}}},

\displaystyle=\operatorname{dec}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}}({\mbox{\boldmath{$z$}}})_{i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.88266pt}{\resizebox{659088}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{570935}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87804pt}{\resizebox{658648}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.375pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathstrut$}}}}}}}.

Like the decoder, the GFN-ROM model at inference in the expansive case can be thought of as a neural operator, but with the mapper acting as the lifting function. Thus, in such settings, GFN-ROM encoder and decoder are very closely linked to resolution-invariant neural operators with a choice of piecewise constant kernel function.

6.2.3.
ROM-related properties

In addition to the above proofs, the GFN-ROM architecture benefits from a number of typical properties important for reduced order models. Namely, it is purely data-driven and non-intrusive, not requiring any knowledge of the generation process for the training data. It is furthermore a nonlinear method, leveraging an autoencoder for nonlinear compression. The method is suitable for unstructured meshes, which is vital in order to deal with general PDEs, where the presence of complex domains typically lead to unstructured meshes. Most importantly, the method is a completely multifidelity approach and is capable of inference on arbitrary meshes.

7.
Adaptive Multifidelity Training

The GFN method proposes a means of transferring weights from one graph
\mathcal{M}_{o}
to a new graph
\mathcal{M}_{n}
as
\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{e},\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}}^{d},\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}}^{d}=\operatorname{GFN}^{\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}}({\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e},{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d},{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{d})
, allowing one to evaluate a feedforward-based approach on new meshes. Whilst this is already a major advantage, it does not prescribe how to effectively train in a multifidelity setting.

In this section, we discuss means of training in multifidelity applications. We aim at learning from a series of
T
meshes
\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1},\cdots,\mathcal{M}_{n}^{T}
by training the GFN-ROM model with weights and biases corresponding to a fixed or an adaptive mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
. We describe below the two approaches, the difference between which is illustrated in
Figure 9
.

7.1.
Fixed mesh

The GFN transforms are entirely differentiable, meaning that for weights and biases
{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e}
,
{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d}
and
{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{d}
associated on a fixed mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
, it is possible to compute gradients
\nabla_{\bm{W}^{e}}\mathcal{L}
,
\nabla_{\bm{W}^{d}}\mathcal{L}
and
\nabla_{\bm{b}^{d}}\mathcal{L}
when training on new meshes. As a result, it is in fact possible to use the standard gradient-based optimisation methods to learn the optimal weights
{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{e}
,
{\mbox{\boldmath{$W$}}}^{d}
and
{\mbox{\boldmath{$b$}}}^{d}
, which are always associated to the mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
. If one considers the mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
as being a suitable sampling of the domain, this approach therefore suffices as a means of training the model in a multifidelity setting. However, the choice of mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
is extremely important for the performance of the model. The fixed nature of
\mathcal{M}_{o}
is also undesirable since, like interpolation approaches onto fixed grids, it does not allow the model to adapt the mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
to better match the training data it sees.

7.2.
Adaptive mesh

GFN-ROM differs from interpolation onto a fixed grid since it is possible to transform weights and biases from a coarse mesh into weights and biases for a finer mesh. Thus, one can always overwrite the old weights and biases corresponding to the old mesh with new weights and biases of the new mesh, and then optimise the new weights. As a result, the approach leads to a number of advantages in allowing the architecture to adapt its number of parameters during training. That is, instead of having a single fixed mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
associated to the fixed weights and biases for the model, we replace it by a master mesh
\mathcal{M}^{t}
which can change during each training sample
t=1,\cdots,T
. Importantly, the sequence of master meshes during training must be hierarchically increasing i.e.
\mathcal{M}^{0}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{M}^{T}
, since we do not wish to lose information at any point.

A simple scheme for adaptive training could be setting
\mathcal{M}^{t}=\mathcal{M}^{t-1}\cup\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}
. However, this approach is suboptimal since it necessarily adds every node seen during training to the master mesh. This means that the master mesh can become prohibitively large, and result in a worse time complexity of
O((N_{o}+N_{n})\log N_{o}+(N_{o}+N_{n})\log N_{n})
for the transform
\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}
.

Instead, a better approach is possible without incurring the computational issues of the previous algorithm. As discussed in
subsubsection 5.2.3
and summarised in
Algorithm
3
, the GFN transforms can be decomposed into an expansive and agglomerative part via the choice of a suitable intermediate mesh given in
Equation 13
. This intermediate mesh is already computed during the GFN transforms, meaning it does not represent an extra operation. Furthermore, the intermediate mesh is larger than the original mesh. Therefore, it is possible to use such mesh to develop a computationally efficient adaptive approach i.e.,
\mathcal{M}^{t}=\mathcal{M}^{t-1}\cup\{\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\left[i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\right]\text{ s.t. }j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{930979}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{t-1}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1006355}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}^{t-1}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1043148}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}^{t-1}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1107869}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}^{t-1}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\leftarrow i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\text{ but not }j_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{930979}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{t-1}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1006355}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}^{t-1}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1043148}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}^{t-1}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1107869}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}^{t-1}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\rightarrow i_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.07169pt}{\resizebox{746991}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16052pt}{\resizebox{808913}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.30884pt}{\resizebox{827713}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.735pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.16153pt}{\resizebox{842306}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{t}\mathstrut$}}}}}}\}
.

Figure 9
.
Training with fixed or adaptive mesh methods when given a starting mesh
\mathcal{M}_{o}
and new mesh
\mathcal{M}_{n}
. The fixed mesh approach updates the original mesh weights, whilst the adaptive mesh approach updates weights on a new mesh
\mathcal{M}
, created from information from both meshes.

For a transform
\mathcal{M}_{o}\to\mathcal{M}_{n}
, we retain the time complexity of the non-adaptive algorithm. Furthermore, this approach only adds nodes to the master mesh when the master mesh undersamples
6
6

6

Undersampling here refers purely to nearest-neighbour information i.e., areas where a node in the master mesh has multiple nearest neighbours are considered undersampled.
. We note that because the weights and biases are changing shape along with the master mesh during training for adaptive methods, one cannot use Adam, RMSProp or any momentum-based optimisation methods for updates
7
7

7

In practice, we often know all of the training fidelities we shall see during training. In such cases, the final mesh
\mathcal{M}^{T}
can be precomputed and the model trained as for a fixed mesh.
.

, illustrating the difference between the adaptive and fixed mesh modes.

8.
Numerical Simulations

Having introduced the GFN-ROM architecture, providing guarantees on super- and sub-resolution errors, we examine its performance on three challenging benchmarks for MOR. We compare the results of the methodology to intrusive and non-intrusive ROMs: namely, POD-Galerkin (POD-G) and POD with projection (representing the best possible linear method), POD-NN
[
47
,
76
]
and GCA-ROM
[
77
]
. For fairness, we strive to make the MOR baselines for the different models as comparable as possible by adopting similar architectural and hyperparameter choices between methods, as shown in
Appendix
B.1
. Importantly, we do not fine tune the architecture or seek the best possible initialisation for each specific problem. As such, we stress that these results do not represent the best possible performance, but illustrate that GFN-ROM is able to directly handle several challenging benchmarks with no specific fine tuning. As a general rule, we have chosen a latent/reduced dimension
N,L=\lfloor 1.5\times N_{\mu}\rfloor
, and the small-data regime with a train/test split of 30/70.

Each of the problems examined here are selected to showcase a different physical or computational complexity. The first two benchmarks have been previously investigated with GCA-ROM
[
77
]
, which we use as a baseline for nonlinear reduction – namely the Graetz problem and an advection-dominated problem. Furthermore, we examine a Stokes flow benchmark with a 7-dimensional parameter space to illustrate the model’s capability of dealing with high-dimensional physical and geometric parameter spaces, and the low data regime with sparse samplings of the parameter space. Training data is generated via finite element methods using the RBniCS package
[
90
]
, which is built on top of the FEniCS package
[
1
,
67
]
. For each problem, we consider four meshes in order to test the multifidelity performance of our architecture
8
8

8

The meshes are generated from the largest mesh by using the moving front subsampling algorithm
[
61
]
.
.

8.1.
Graetz problem

The Graetz problem represents a common benchmark in MOR, combining a forced heat diffusion with horizontal heat advection in a parametrised geometry. Specifically, we study a steady-state Graetz problem on the parametrised geometry
\Omega(\mu_{1})=\Omega^{1}\cup\Omega^{2}(\mu_{1})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, with
\Omega^{1}=[0,1]\times[0,1]
and
\Omega^{2}(\mu_{1})=[1,1+\mu_{1}]\times[0,1]
illustrated in
10(a)
, and discretised by four meshes as summarised in Table
3
.

(a)

(b)

Figure 10
.
(A) The parameterised domain for the Graetz problem. (B) Mean relative errors for GFN-ROM trained on the large mesh for the Graetz problem. Squares indicate the parameter realisations seen during training.

Homogeneous Dirichlet conditions are imposed on
\Gamma_{D}=\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{5}\cup\Gamma_{6}
, whilst a constant Dirichlet boundary condition is imposed on
\Gamma_{G}=\Gamma_{2}(\mu_{1})\cup\Gamma_{4}(\mu_{1})
as
u(\bm{\mu})|_{\Gamma_{G}}=1
. The remaining boundary
\Gamma_{3}(\mu_{1})
is given a homogeneous von Neumann boundary condition. The weak formulation of the Graetz problem reads as follows: given
\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{P}
, find
u(\bm{\mu})\in\mathbb{U}(\mu_{1})

s.t.

(32)

\displaystyle\mu_{2}\int_{\Omega(\mu_{1})}\nabla u\cdot\nabla v\,d\Omega+\int_{\Omega(\mu_{1})}y(1-y)\frac{\partial u}{\partial x}v\,d\Omega=0,\quad\forall v\in\mathbb{V},

where
\mathbb{U}(\mu_{1})=\{v\in H^{1}(\Omega(\mu_{1})):v|_{\Gamma_{D}}=0,\penalty\ v|_{\Gamma_{G}}=1\}
and
\mathbb{V}(\mu_{1})=\{v\in H^{1}(\Omega(\mu_{1})):v|_{\Gamma_{D}\cup\Gamma_{G}}=0\}
. Note that due to the parametrised domain, these function spaces are dependent on the geometric parameter.
This problem is parametrised by
\bm{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
, where
\mu_{1}
is a geometrical parameter governing the length of
\Omega^{2}
, and
\mu_{2}
is a physical parameter, namely the diffusivity coefficient. We consider a dataset with 200 snapshots computed on a uniform grid of
\mathbb{P}
, with 10 and 20 equispaced values for
\mu_{1}\in[1,3]
and
\mu_{2}\in[0.01,0.1]
, respectively.

Training and testing the performance on GFN-ROM on the largest mesh, we achieve a mean relative error of 1.02% over the full dataset. As illustrated in
10(b)
